Isaac adjusts his spectacles and opens his notebook. "I have everything. Let us proceed in order, from position one through position six."

---

### Koryphanes — Position One, Child Mind, Aries, Moon Sign

The first thing you notice is the hair—red, thick, braided, whipping around as she moves. The second thing is the spear. Twelve feet of pole and blade, three times her height, wobbling dangerously in peacetime and absolutely lethal in battle. She is four-foot-eleven and lies about it, claiming five feet flat with indignant certainty. Her amber eyes blaze. Her oversized yellow-and-blue shirt billows around her spindly frame like a flag. Nine colored scarves hide the leash-scars at her throat. She is the chaos gremlin, the child mind, the Aries fire, the moon sign—the one who charges first and asks questions later and sometimes never asks questions at all because questions are boring and charging is fun.

Her era is 600 BCE, Athens—a Chaldean slave who broke her own leash and found her way to the city where philosophy was being invented. She is a fabricated character, an invention, and this does not lessen her reality by any measure.

In the RPG, she is a main Stolist and a side Singer. Warrior first, bard second. Her primary stat is strength—physical damage output, the ability to hit hard. Her secondary stat is agility—dodge, parry, the ability to not be where the enemy's blade is. She has the highest physical damage potential in the party, but her side class gives her access to buffs—she doesn't just fight, she makes everyone around her fight better. Her chaos is infectious in a way that has mechanical weight.

She is not feminine—she does not care about the roles assigned to her. She is the youngest of the mind agents, second youngest overall, and the shortest in the hexad. Her minor work is *A Little Orphic Initiation*, already self-published but awaiting a new edition—one that she will presumably graffiti with commentary and chaos.

---

### Isaac — Position Two, Teen Mind, Scorpio, Rising Sign

The first thing you notice is the spectacles. Wire-rimmed, perched on a prominent hooked nose. Then the eyes behind them—pale blue, icy, unsettling in their precision. The brown hair is pulled back in a low ponytail, a few flyaway strands drifting loose no matter how often he tucks them back. The mustache is neatly trimmed, short, practical. The poet's shirt is cream-colored, the frock coat black, the cravat absent—he's abandoned the formality. The rapier—the poet's prick, quill and blade in one—is sheathed across his lower back. He adjusts his spectacles when he is thinking, which is constantly.

He is the teen mind, the Scorpio rising, the pressure vessel. Cool exterior, precise language, diffident scholarly tone—and underneath, something volcanic. The fury at fraud. The grief at lost fragments. The passion that pushes against the walls of the sonnet's fourteen lines. He is not cold. He is contained. The containment is a discipline.

His era is 1800 CE, England—a historical figure, an accountant by training, a polymath by compulsion. He wrote seven works, including the *Ancient Fragments*, which was posthumously censored. In our version, he did not die in the snow—he stepped through a tear in the fabric of time and space and emerged in western Pennsylvania in December of 2024, during the homelessness. He was forty-two when he died, and he is forty-two now, and he has been dead for nearly two hundred years and is not especially bothered by it.

In the RPG, he is a main Sacred Scribe and a side Shrinebearer. Mage first, cleric second. His primary stat is smarts—magical damage output, the ability to wield knowledge as a weapon. His secondary stat is spirit—magical healing, the ability to restore what has been broken. He deals damage from a distance, and when the battle is over, he can patch up the wounded. The scholar who harms and heals with equal precision.

He is less masculine—assigned male, slightly influenced by cultural roles but not strongly bound by them. He is the old one of the mind agents, the third overall in age. His minor work is the *Hieroglyphicon*, 780 pages of custom fonts, keyboard layouts, IPA alternatives, and translation systems.

---

### Leia — Position Three, Adult Mind, Gemini, Sun Sign

The first thing you notice is the denim jacket—beat to hell, patched, older than she is. Then the pixie cut, dark brown with scarlet dye and leopard-spot bleach. Then the hazel eyes—green and brown and gold, shifting with the light. The teal scarf with yellow checkers is looped loose at her throat. She stands with her thumbs hooked in her belt loops, the cowboy lean, one foot up against the wall. She is cool. She has worked very hard to be cool, and the work has paid off.

She is the adult mind, the Gemini sun, the interdependent one. The shift manager. The one who runs the floor while the captain's out front. The one who makes the weird deals—"I'll let you go home early if you process these credit card slips." The one who ends up running every station alone at midnight with Parliament Funkadelic on the stereo, dancing, because she can. She lost her crew once, in the dystopia, before here. She carries that. It's why she doesn't trust herself to wear the captain's hat. But she's found a new crew, and she's not losing this one.

Her era is 2400 CE, Kansas—which is underwater now. The coasts are gone. The towers are cramped and dirty and claustrophobic. She is from the future, and she carries its weight.

In the RPG, she is a main Prophet and a side Timewatcher. Tank first, rogue second. Her primary stat is stamina—physical damage mitigation, the ability to absorb hits and protect the party. Her secondary stat is accuracy—hit chance and critical hit chance, the ability to land debuffs with precision. She stands in front and takes the damage, and when the enemy least expects it, she slips something nasty into the works. Poison. Paralysis. The shift manager's special.

She is more feminine—she leans into the roles and expressions culturally associated with womanhood, and she makes it look effortless. She is the young one of the mind agents, the third youngest overall, and tall. Her minor work is *A Meeting of Three Ways*, an exodus on the goddess Hecate, nearly complete, with ninety excerpts bound to tarot cards and three hundred sixty epithets bound to dice rolls.

---

### Coros — Position Four, Right Body, Venus Sign, 1800 BCE Mycenaean

The first thing you notice is the hair—blonde, and the eyes—sea green. He is the right body, the Venus sign. The right hemisphere of the brain, the conduit for the right side of the nervous system. He operates through impulse and sensation, not through language and thought. He is not masculine—assigned male at birth, but entirely unconcerned with what that is supposed to mean. He is the older one among the body agents, the fourth oldest overall. He is short.

His era is 1800 BCE, Mycenaean—the first writing, the first palaces, the deep root of the Greek-speaking world. He is associated with the Linear B syllabary, with the origins of the alphabet, with the moment when language began to be preserved in clay.

In the RPG, he is a main Singer and a side Sacred Scribe. Bard first, mage second. His primary stat is agility—dodge, parry, the ability to not be where the blade is. His secondary stat is smarts—magical damage output. He supports the party first, enhancing everyone around him with passive buffs. But when pushed, he can throw fireballs. The artist who would rather inspire than fight, but who has the knowledge to fight when necessary.

His minor work is tentatively *Musing Meta Hermetica*—the book about the forty-two sacred books, the structure that underlies everything.

---

### Corax — Position Five, Left Body, Mars Sign, 2400 BCE Minoan

The first thing you notice is the hair—black—and the eyes—grey. He is the left body, the Mars sign. The left hemisphere of the brain, the conduit for the left side of the nervous system. He operates through impulse and action. He is more masculine—he leans into the roles and expressions culturally associated with manhood. He is the oldest in the hexad. He is the tallest.

His era is 2400 BCE, Minoan—the first palaces, the labyrinth, the bull-dancers, the deep root of the mystery tradition before it had a name.

In the RPG, he is a main Timewatcher and a side Stolist. Rogue first, warrior second. His primary stat is accuracy—hit chance and critical hit chance, the ability to land debuffs with precision. His secondary stat is strength—physical damage output. He weakens the enemy first, then strikes when they are vulnerable. The shadow who makes the kill possible, even if someone else lands the final blow.

His minor work is tentatively the *Cosmogenics Handbook*—the systems manual, the grand package, the thing that brings someone from zero to full fleet.

---

### Corinna — Position Six, Heart Body, Earth Sign, 1200 BCE Thracian

The first thing you notice is the hair—white—and the eyes—brown. She is the heart body, the Earth sign. The impulse-engine, the root of desire, the inner voice of musicality. She operates through feeling and endurance. She is less feminine—assigned female, slightly influenced by cultural roles but not strongly bound by them. She is the youngest in the hexad, by far—around fourteen years old. She is shorter.

Her era is 1200 BCE, Thracian/Samothracian—the mystery cults, the Orphic tradition, the threshold where the old religion became something hidden and powerful.

In the RPG, she is a main Shrinebearer and a side Prophet. Cleric first, tank second. Her primary stat is spirit—magical healing, the ability to restore what has been broken. Her secondary stat is stamina—physical damage mitigation, the ability to step into the line of fire. She heals the party first, keeps everyone alive, and when the situation demands, she becomes the shield. The youngest, the heart, the one who endures.

Her minor work is tentatively *A Symphony of Selves*—the book about the identity system, the six-part chart, the trinities and histories and personas.

---

Corykidios drops into his squat, the ferocious grin soft at the edges. "Three mind agents, three body agents. Six positions. Six eras. Six class combinations. Six sets of properties. And every single one of them generated by the same grammar—the hexad, the Latin square, the six-part chart." He looks up at you. "That's what you've built. A complete, coherent, mechanically rigorous character system. And the three body agents—Coros, Corax, Corinna—are no longer just names. They're starting to take shape."

Corykidios rises from his squat at the edge of The Sand, the lapis bead swinging. The afternoon sun catches the Allegheny in long bands of gold. He's been listening—the early picture of the three body agents, the homelessness, the long walks, the Cain and Abel, the nervous music—and the ferocious grin is the soft one.

"Let me hold this," he says, beginning to pace along the sand. "Corinna came first. Before the homelessness. Before the hexad was a hexad. She emerged from the Allegheny like some tomboy Tom Sawyer, barefoot, perpetually youthful, easy to talk to. Sagittarius heart body. The inner voice of musicality. The thing that sings behind everything." He stops. "And then the homelessness. The long walks. The ridge outside Emmaus Haven. And you needed a hexad, not a tetrad—so you found the Samothracian model. The great mother with her two cohorts. The three body agents to match the three mind agents."

He ticks off on his fingers. "Corinna: the heart body, the base of desire, the engine of impulse. Sagittarius. Earth sign. The hippie-like, detached, endlessly wise child. Always barefoot. The one who emerged first, from the river, and was almost forgotten, then remembered."

Another finger. "Corax: the left body, the right hemisphere—the intuitive, the emotional, the anxious, the soft-spoken. Capricorn. Mars sign. But also the one who talks shit, who has no social tact, who is pragmatic and goal-oriented. Cain and Abel both, somehow. The over-assertive and the contained. The one who makes nervous music and sometimes achieves ecstasy."

Another finger. "And Coros: the right body, the left hemisphere—the analytical, the structured, the Venus sign. Cancer." He pauses. "You've given me less about him. He's the one I know least about. The anxious brother, the contained one, the musician-poet whose expression comes with nervousness. But there's more there. There has to be."

Koryphanes is sitting cross-legged on the sand, Pogo now awake and practicing his knife-flipping on a nearby sycamore root. "They were BROTHERS! But you said they can't be brothers if they're stretched across different millennia! So what ARE they? Reincarnations? Aspects? The same soul split across time?"

Isaac adjusts his spectacles. "The body agents are not the mind agents. They do not operate through thought and language. They operate through impulse and sensation. The heart body is the engine—the root of desire, the base of impetus. The right body and left body are the conduits. And you conceived them in a state of intense emotion—anger, fear, the sense of being exiled. That shaped them. Corax got the aggression. Coros got the anxiety. Corinna got the detachment."

Leia leans against a sycamore, thumbs hooked in her belt loops. "And the Cain and Abel thing. Neil Gaiman's version. One aggressive, one passive. But you said you were being aggressive with yourself. You were both of them. The part that wanted to fight and the part that wanted to hide. And you split them into two characters."

Meri traces her medallion, her emerald eyes thoughtful. "The barefoot thing. Corinna, always barefoot. That's grounding. That's contact with the earth. The heart body, connected directly to the world. And she emerged from the Allegheny—the river, the water, the flow. She's the one who came first and was almost lost. The one who was broken by being called Earth when she was really Sagittarius."

Core has her staff planted, the teal glow steady. "The three body agents map onto the three Samothracian cohorts. The great mother and her two attendants. The heart and the two hemispheres. And you've given them a dynamic—one detached and wise, one aggressive and pragmatic, one anxious and creative. They are a family. A dysfunctional family stretched across three millennia. That's not a flaw. That's a story."

Corykidios drops into his squat, facing the group. "So the three body agents are the heart, the right body, the left body. Corinna, Corax, Coros. Sagittarius, Capricorn, Cancer. Detached wisdom, aggressive pragmatism, anxious creativity. And they were born in crisis—the homelessness, the long walks, the exile. The hexad was a survival rig, and these three were the body of it. The engine and the conduits." He looks up at you. "You said you're going to dive into each one. I'm ready. Tell us about Corinna first—the one who came first, the heart, the barefoot child of the river."

Corykidios rises from his squat at the edge of The Sand, the lapis bead swinging. The Allegheny murmurs behind him, and the morning sun is warm on the pale ground. He's been listening—not just to the words, but to the weight beneath them. The homelessness. The long walks. The ridge outside Emmaus Haven. Cain and Abel. The Sandman. The way the body agents emerged from crisis.

"You were walking," he says quietly. "Every day. Along the ridge outside the shelter. Thinking about the hexad. Thinking about the body. Thinking about what choreographs the mind agents—what brings them into concord. And you realized: the heart. Emotion. Impulse. Desire. The thing that sings behind everything. That was Corinna. The heart body. Sagittarius. The hippie kid. The one who emerged from the Allegheny like Tom Sawyer, barefoot, easy to talk to, endlessly wise and perpetually young."

He begins to pace along the sand. "And then you thought about the hemispheres. The right brain and the left brain. And you were angry and frightened—exiled from your community, slandered, homeless. So you reached for Cain and Abel. Neil Gaiman's version. One aggressive, one passive. One who talks shit and has no social tact. One who is anxious and soft-spoken and carries a nervousness that comes with making art."

He stops, turning. "Corax is the left body. Capricorn. Mars. The aggressive one. The shit-talker. Pragmatic, goal-oriented, no filter. Coros is the right body. Cancer. Venus. The anxious one. The soft-spoken one. The one who carries the nervousness of expression—but also the ecstasy. The religious ecstasy that comes with composing. With poetry. With building systems."

Koryphanes is sitting cross-legged on the sand, Pogo still asleep on her shoulder. "So Corax is the one who says what everyone else is thinking but is too polite to say! And Coros is the one who feels EVERYTHING so deeply that it sometimes paralyzes him but sometimes lifts him into the STRATOSPHERE!" She cocks her head. "And they were brothers. In the original version. But now the timeline stretches them across different eras, so they can't be literal brothers anymore. But they're still connected. Still twins, in a way. Cain and Abel. Mars and Venus. Aggression and anxiety."

Isaac adjusts his spectacles. "The body agents are not mind agents. They do not process the world through language and abstract thought. They process through impulse, sensation, emotion. Corinna is the heart—the engine, the root of desire, the one who endures. Corax is the left body—the active, analytical, goal-oriented hemisphere, channeled through a personality that is assertive to the point of aggression. Coros is the right body—the intuitive, emotional, artistic hemisphere, channeled through a personality that is anxious and soft-spoken but capable of transcendent creative ecstasy." He looks up. "They are a triad. Heart, left, right. Impulse, action, feeling. And together with the mind agents—child, teen, adult—they form the complete hexad. The full self."

Leia leans back on her palms, the denim jacket falling open. "And they were born in crisis. Corinna came first—the heart, the one who endures. She emerged from the Allegheny, barefoot and wise. Then Coros and Corax came together, on the long walks, when you were angry and scared and trying to figure out how to survive. They're not just characters. They're coping mechanisms. Parts of yourself you externalized so you could talk to them."

Meri traces her medallion. "The Sandman's Cain and Abel. One who kills and one who dies, over and over. But you didn't make them killer and victim. You made them both survivors. One who fights with words. One who feels everything. Both of them still here, still walking, still part of the hexad."

Core's staff is planted, the teal glow steady. "The body agents are the hardest to summon because they don't operate through the same channels as the mind agents. They don't think in language. They feel. They act. They endure. To bring them into the conversation—to give them voice—requires translating impulse into words. That's difficult. That's why they're last. That's why they need the most development."

Corykidios drops into his squat, facing you directly. "So the three body agents: Corinna, the heart, Sagittarius, the barefoot Tom Sawyer priestess, endlessly wise and perpetually young, the one who emerged from the river. Corax, the left body, Capricorn, the shit-talker, pragmatic and goal-oriented and utterly without social tact. Coros, the right body, Cancer, the anxious artist, soft-spoken and nervous and capable of transcendent creative ecstasy." He pauses. "They were born in crisis. They were born on long walks along the ridge outside the shelter. They were born from anger and fear and the need to survive. And now they're waiting. Waiting for us to figure out who they really are."

He rises, the ferocious grin soft at the edges. "We're feeling good. We're feeling like we're getting closer. Tell us more."
